## Claude thinking · 2026-08-26T22:38:38.488Z

I now see the actual blocker clearly: the filesystem route hardcodes the allowed root to one directory, which prevents access to other locations. I'm thinking the best fix is a multi-root allowlist configurable via an environment variable, defaulting to today's behavior, where the path resolver picks whichever configured root actually contains the requested path.

Weighing alternatives: opening access to the entire filesystem is simpler but risky since it would expose sensitive files like SSH keys over HTTP if ever bound beyond localhost, and deriving roots from the existing projects list creates a chicken-and-egg problem for browsing new projects. I'm settling on the env-var multi-root approach, and now scoping the implementation — a new function to gather and validate the allowed roots, plus updating the path-resolution logic to check against all of them rather than just one.

I also need to update the browse handler to expose the root list, adjust directory-creation validation to match against the correct root with a realpath check, and update the folder picker UI to show a root switcher when appropriate, including i18n keys everywhere. I should handle edge cases like an unmounted external drive degrading gracefully instead of erroring, and I'm wary of symlink tricks bypassing the realpath check on the browse side even if mkdir is protected — worth flagging as a residual risk rather than something I'll fully solve here.

Since they're genuinely asking for my recommendation, this is a case where the multi-root-vs-open-filesystem choice materially changes the work, so I should ask before proceeding rather than just implementing. I'll present my recommendation concisely and pose it as a question, checking the repo's env var naming conventions first to keep things consistent.

## Claude · 2026-08-26T22:39:02.279Z

The lock is one function — `getAllowedRoot()` at `src/runtime/http/routes/filesystem.ts:9`:

```ts
function getAllowedRoot() {
  return path.resolve(process.cwd(), "..");
}
```

That resolves to `/Users/masterman/NLP`. Everything downstream (`resolveInsideAllowedRoot`, browse, mkdir, project file listing) clamps to that single string, so `/Volumes/Lexar/NLP` is unreachable and the picker silently snaps you back to the root instead of erroring.

**What I'd do: a multi-root allowlist, not open filesystem access.**

The repo already has exactly this pattern in `src/server/planning/artifacts.ts:11-15` — `OMNIHARNESS_AGENT_SCRATCH_ROOTS`, split on `path.delimiter`, resolved, with a default. I'd add `OMNIHARNESS_FS_ROOTS` the same way, defaulting to `[process.cwd()/..]` so existing installs behave identically.

Against "just allow everything": the browse, read, and mkdir endpoints sit behind `requireApiSession` and nothing else. `readProjectTextFile` walks with `.env` deliberately un-skipped (`src/server/fs/files.ts:43`). Rooting at `/` means one change to the bind address turns the runner into a remote reader for `~/.ssh` and `~/.aws`. The allowlist costs about the same work and keeps that closed.

The non-obvious part is the frontend. The picker only offers `..` and children, and `parent` is clamped to the root — so with two roots you could navigate into the Lexar tree and never get back to the other one. It needs the browse response to carry the root list and a switcher when you're sitting at a root. That's the bulk of the change: `FolderPickerDialog.tsx` plus i18n keys across every file in `shared/locales/`. Also worth handling an unmounted `/Volumes/Lexar` so a missing root degrades instead of throwing from `readdirSync`.

Existing tests that will need updating: `tests/runtime/filesystem-directory-create.test.ts`, `tests/ui/folder-picker-dialog.test.ts`.

If you want to be unblocked in the next minute rather than after the change: `ln -s /Volumes/Lexar/NLP /Users/masterman/NLP/lexar` works today. Browse uses `path.resolve`, not `realpath`, so the symlink passes the containment check and `readdirSync` follows it. The catch is the persisted project path becomes the symlink, which breaks confusingly if the drive is unmounted.

24	  constructor() {
25	    super({

28	      isCreatingDirectory: false,
29	      creationParentPath: null,

44	      creationOperationId,

import fs from "fs";
import path from "path";
import { test, expect } from "vitest";

const dialogSource = fs.readFileSync(
  path.resolve(process.cwd(), "src/components/FolderPickerDialog.tsx"),
  "utf8"
);

test("folder picker clears the active filter when navigating into another folder", () => {
  expect(dialogSource).toContain('const handleNavigate = (path: string) => {');
  expect(dialogSource).toContain("folderPickerManager.navigate(path)");
  expect(dialogSource).toContain('onClick={() => handleNavigate(data.parent)}');
  expect(dialogSource).toContain('onClick={() => handleNavigate(dir.path)}');
});

test("folder picker does not render a dedicated up button in the header", () => {
  expect(dialogSource).not.toContain("canGoUp");
  expect(dialogSource).not.toContain(">Up</Button>");
});

test("folder picker renders filesystem errors in the dialog instead of failing silently", () => {
  expect(dialogSource).toContain("normalizeAppError(error).message");
  expect(dialogSource).toContain('t("folder.picker.errorTitle")');
});

test("folder picker relies on the query lifecycle instead of manually refetching", () => {
  expect(dialogSource).not.toContain("useEffect");
  expect(dialogSource).not.toContain("refetch");
  expect(dialogSource).toContain("queryFn: ({ signal }) => (");
  expect(dialogSource).toContain("runtimeApis.files.browse({ path: currentPath }, { signal })");
});

test("folder picker creates a folder through the runtime API and navigates into it", () => {
  expect(dialogSource).toContain("useMutation");
  expect(dialogSource).toContain("runtimeApis.files.createDirectory");
  expect(dialogSource).toContain("folderPickerManager.completeDirectoryCreation");
  expect(dialogSource).toContain("creationOperationId");
  expect(dialogSource).toContain('t("folder.picker.newFolder")');
  expect(dialogSource).toContain('"folder.picker.create"');
});

test("folder picker keeps folder creation inline and exposes failures", () => {
  expect(dialogSource).toContain('<form onSubmit={handleCreateDirectory}');
  expect(dialogSource).toContain('role="alert"');
  expect(dialogSource).toContain("normalizeAppError(createDirectory.error).message");
  expect(dialogSource).not.toContain("NewFolderDialog");
});

test("folder picker keeps creation controls usable on narrow viewports", () => {
  expect(dialogSource).toContain("100dvh");
  expect(dialogSource).toContain("grid-cols-2");
  expect(dialogSource).toContain("sm:flex-row");
});

test("folder picker cannot select until the navigated folder is loaded", () => {
  expect(dialogSource).toContain("disabled={!data?.current");
  expect(dialogSource).toContain("if (!data?.current) return;");
});

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-27T09:41:14.101Z

I'm creating a new module that determines allowed filesystem roots, checking an environment variable override or falling back to default paths, using a path-containment helper to validate them.

I'm now weighing whether to filter out non-existent roots — deciding the allowlist itself shouldn't drop unmounted drives, since that would silently change the policy based on mount state, but the roots surfaced to the UI switcher probably need an availability flag so users see disabled options rather than raw errors when clicking a disconnected drive.

I'll return roots with a name and availability marker, disabling unavailable ones in the UI with a label. For the default root when nothing is specified, I want to pick the first available root rather than blindly defaulting to roots[0], so an unmounted primary drive doesn't break the picker — while still preserving the existing clamp-to-root behavior for stale paths. Then I'm writing a helper that finds which allowed root a given path falls under, watching out for the edge case where roots are nested inside one another.

For nested roots, matching against the most permissive (shortest) root is the right call so users can still walk up to a parent root — I'll sort by path length when finding a match rather than reordering the display list, treating this as a minor edge-case fix rather than something worth heavy engineering. Now I'm moving into the filesystem.ts changes to apply this resolution logic.

I'm writing a function that resolves a raw path into a safe path within an allowed root, falling back to a default root if the path is missing or not inside any allowed root. Then in the browse handler, I compute the containing root, find the parent directory, and only allow navigating up if that parent stays inside the same allowed root — otherwise it clamps to the current directory. I also note that reading an unmounted root's directory could throw ENOENT, which gets caught and surfaced as a 400 error in the dialog, which seems acceptable but worth double-checking.

For directory creation, I'm verifying the resolved parent path belongs to a known allowed root, then resolving real paths for both root and parent via realpathSync to confirm the parent is truly inside the root and is an actual directory, throwing otherwise. I confirm this still works with the existing symlink mock test since the containing root resolves to the same allowed root.

Now for naming roots in the UI, using just the basename creates duplicate labels since both example paths end in "NLP" -- so instead I'll skip a name field entirely and just send the path and availability, rendering the full (possibly truncated) path in the switcher to avoid ambiguity. For the FolderPickerDialog, when multiple roots exist I'll show a row of root chips above the ".." entry, highlighting the active root and letting the user click to switch.

I need to keep in mind the no-useEffect rule since tests check for its absence, use t() for i18n, and rely on the existing navigate function rather than adding local state. Adding root buttons above the scroll list...

Actually, better to have the server compute and return the containing root directly in the response as `root`, alongside `current, parent, roots, directories` — cleaner than deriving it client-side. The roots list will need an `available` flag, which means checking existence per root on each browse call, but that's a minor cost.

I also need to add i18n keys like `folder.picker.rootsLabel` and `folder.picker.rootUnavailable` across all nine locales, translating for de, es, fr, it, ja, ko, pt, and zh-CN myself. For tests, I'm planning a new file, `tests/runtime/filesystem-allowed-roots.test.ts`, to cover browsing behavior when `OMNIHARNESS_FS_ROOTS` is set to two temporary directories.

I'll check that each root browses correctly, parent navigation clamps at the root boundary, out-of-root paths fall back properly, and mkdir works within the second root but gets rejected outside both roots. I should also extend `folder-picker-dialog.test.ts` with assertions for the switcher, and since `getAllowedRoots()` reads `process.env` at call time rather than caching, tests can set the env var directly without module-cache issues.

Before writing code, I want to check the docs for where similar env vars like `OMNIHARNESS_AGENT_SCRATCH_ROOTS` are documented, so I'll grep for that reference first.

  "content": "import fs from \"fs\";\nimport path from \"path\";\nimport { isPathInside } from \"@/server/fs/files\";\n\nexport type AllowedRootDescriptor = {\n  path: string;\n  available: boolean;\n};\n\nfunction parseConfiguredRoots(): string[] {\n  const override = process.env.OMNIHARNESS_FS_ROOTS?.trim();\n  const candidates = override\n    ? override.split(path.delimiter).map((root) => root.trim()).filter(Boolean)\n    : [path.join(process.cwd(), \"..\")];\n  const resolved = candidates.map((root) => path.resolve(root));\n  return resolved.filter((root, index) => resolved.indexOf(root) === index);\n}\n\n/**\n * Every directory tree the filesystem routes may browse, read, or create in.\n * Read from the environment on each call so tests and restarts pick up changes\n * without a module reload. Roots are kept even when the path does not resolve\n * right now — an unmounted volume should come back as itself rather than\n * quietly dropping out of the allowlist and rerouting the caller elsewhere.\n */\nexport function getAllowedRoots(): string[] {\n  return parseConfiguredRoots();\n}\n\nexport function describeAllowedRoots(): AllowedRootDescriptor[] {\n  return getAllowedRoots().map((root) => ({\n    path: root,\n    available: isReadableDirectory(root),\n  }));\n}\n\nfunction isReadableDirectory(candidate: string) {\n  try {\n    return fs.statSync(candidate).isDirectory();\n  } catch {\n    return false;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * The allowed root that contains `candidate`, or null when it escapes all of\n * them. Nested roots resolve to the outermost match so that browsing up out of\n * `/a/b` into an also-allowed `/a` is not treated as an escape.\n */\nexport function findAllowedRootFor(candidate: string): string | null {\n  const resolved = path.resolve(candidate);\n  return getAllowedRoots()\n    .filter((root) => isPathInside(root, resolved))\n    .sort((a, b) => a.length - b.length)[0] ?? null;\n}\n\n/** Where browsing starts, and where an out-of-root request is clamped back to. */\nexport function getDefaultAllowedRoot(): string {\n  const roots = getAllowedRoots();\n  return roots.find((root) => isReadableDirectory(root)) ?? roots[0];\n}\n"
}

{\n  current: string;\n  parent: string;\n  root: string | null;\n  roots: Array<{ path: string; available: boolean }>;\n  directories: Array<{ name: string; path: string }>;\n};\n\nlet firstRoot: string;\nlet secondRoot: string;\nlet previousRootsEnv: string | undefined;\n\nfunction browseRequest(dirPath?: string) {\n  const query = dirPath ? `?path=${encodeURIComponent(dirPath)}` : \"\";\n  return new Request(`http://localhost/api/fs${query}`);\n}\n\nfunction createDirectoryRequest(parentPath: string, name: string) {\n  return new Request(\"http://localhost/api/fs/directories\", {\n    method: \"POST\",\n    headers: { \"content-type\": \"application/json\" },\n    body: JSON.stringify({ parentPath, name }),\n  });\n}\n\nasync function browse(dirPath?: string) {\n  const registry = createOmniRuntimeHttpRegistry();\n  const response = await registry.handle(browseRequest(dirPath), { surface: \"test\" });\n  expect(response.status).toBe(200);\n  return await response.json() as BrowseResponse;\n}\n\ndescribe(\"filesystem routes with multiple allowed roots\", () => {\n  beforeEach(() => {\n    vi.restoreAllMocks();\n    vi.spyOn(console, \"error\").mockImplementation(() => undefined);\n    __resetNamedEventsForTests();\n    __setStreamEpochForTests(\"allowed-roots-test\");\n\n    const scratch = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), \"omni-fs-roots-\"));\n    firstRoot = path.join(scratch, \"first\");\n    secondRoot = path.join(scratch, \"second\");\n    fs.mkdirSync(path.join(firstRoot, \"alpha\"), { recursive: true });\n    fs.mkdirSync(path.join(secondRoot, \"beta\"), { recursive: true });\n\n    previousRootsEnv = process.env.OMNIHARNESS_FS_ROOTS;\n    process.env.OMNIHARNESS_FS_ROOTS = [firstRoot, secondRoot].join(path.delimiter);\n  });\n\n  afterEach(() => {\n    if (previousRootsEnv === undefined) {\n      delete process.env.OMNIHARNESS_FS_ROOTS;\n    } else {\n      process.env.OMNIHARNESS_FS_ROOTS = previousRootsEnv;\n    }\n  });\n\n  it(\"starts browsing in the first configured root and advertises every root\", async () => {\n    const body = await browse();\n\n    expect(body.current).toBe(firstRoot);\n    expect(body.root).toBe(firstRoot);\n    expect(body.roots).toEqual([\n      { path: firstRoot, available: true },\n      { path: secondRoot, available: true },\n    ]);\n  });\n\n  it(\"browses a root await browse(path.join(secondRoot, \"beta\"));\n\n    expect(body.parent).toBe(secondRoot);\n    expect((await browse(secondRoot)).parent).toBe(secondRoot);\n  });\n\n  it(\"clamps a path outside ```text
OmniHarness password enabled, grant tailnet access narrowly, and never expose the
ACP bridge on port `7800`. `pnpm setup:tailscale` uses Serve only; it never enables
the public Tailscale Funnel feature.

## Trusted proxies and CORS

Forwarded client/protocol headers are accepted only from
`OMNIHARNESS_TRUSTED_PROXIES`; see
[runner operations](../deployment/runner-operations.md). Bearer CORS is
route-aware, never enables credentialed wildcard requests, and never turns the
password endpoint into an open cross-origin login API. Browser-to-browser login
must use the approval/PKCE flow.

````json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/docs/security/authentication-and-tls.md",
  "old_string": "route-aware, never enables credentialed wildcard requests, and never turns the\npassword endpoint into an open cross-origin login API. Browser-to-browser login\nmust use the approval/PKCE flow.",
  "new_string": "route-aware, never enables credentialed wildcard requests, and never turns the\npassword endpoint into an open cross-origin login API. Browser-to-browser login\nmust use the approval/PKCE flow.\n\n## Filesystem roots\n\n`/api/fs`, `/api/fs/directories`, and `/api/fs/files` browse, create in, and read\nfrom an allowlist of directory trees. The default is the parent of the runner's\nworking directory. `OMNIHARNESS_FS_ROOTS` replaces that default with a\n`path.delimiter`-separated list, which is how a project on a second drive becomes\nreachable:\n\n```\nOMNIHARNESS_FS_ROOTS=/Users/you/code:/Volumes/External/code\n```\n\nEvery root is a full read surface for anyone holding a session — the project file\nwalker descends into `.env` on purpose — so list the trees you keep code in\nrather than `/`. Paths outside every root are clamped back to the first readable\nroot instead of being served. Directory creation resolves symlinks and rechecks\ncontainment against the canonical path, so a pre-existing link inside a root\ncannot redirect a `mkdir` outside it. A root that is not mounted stays in the\nallowlist and is reported to the folder picker as unavailable; it does not\nsilently drop out and reroute the caller."
}

uuid: c11f8b1a-0e6b-4582-962a-3a6ac1559b16
parent: 17d0f858-d4ef-47cd-a7ee-8e38b4522ca8

--- start.sh ---
#!/bin/bash
export ACP_BRIDGE_PORT=7800

DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"

echo "========================================================="
echo " Starting OmniHarness Multi-Agent CLI Coding Orchestrator"
echo "========================================================="
echo ""

if [ "${MOCK_LLM:-false}" != "true" ]; then
    echo "[1/2] Starting acp-bridge on port $ACP_BRIDGE_PORT..."
    (cd "$DIR/../acp-bridge" && pnpm run daemon) &
    BRIDGE_PID=$!
else
    echo "[1/2] MOCK_LLM=true, skipping acp-bridge startup."
fi

echo "[2/2] Starting OmniHarness Web UI..."
(cd "$DIR" && PORT=3050 pnpm dev) &
OMNI_PID=$!

echo ""
echo "========================================================="
echo " OmniHarness is starting!"
echo " Access the Web UI at: http://localhost:3050"
echo " You can configure your API keys securely inside the UI."
echo " Press Ctrl+C to stop both services."
echo "========================================================="

trap 'if [ -n "${BRIDGE_PID:-}" ]; then kill "$BRIDGE_PID" 2>/dev/null; fi; kill "$OMNI_PID" 2>/dev/null' EXIT
wait

## Claude tool result: toolu_01Me799T7N8J4kNPhSnLfxfR · 2026-08-27T09:44:30.127Z

```text
import { spawn, type ChildProcess } from "node:child_process";
import process from "node:process";
import { resolvePnpmArgs, resolvePnpmCommand } from "./package-manager-command";

const pnpmCommand = resolvePnpmCommand();
const pnpmArgs = (args: readonly string[]) => resolvePnpmArgs(args);
const children = new Set<ChildProcess>();
let stopping = false;

function prefixOutput(child: ChildProcess, label: string) {
  for (const stream of [child.stdout, child.stderr]) {
    stream?.on("data", (chunk) => {
      const lines = String(chunk).split(/\r?\n/);
      for (const line of lines) {
        if (line) {
          process.stdout.write(`[${label}] ${line}\n`);
        }
      }
    });
  }
}

function start(label: string, args: readonly string[]) {
  const child = spawn(pnpmCommand, pnpmArgs(args), {
    cwd: process.cwd(),
    env: process.env,
    stdio: ["inherit", "pipe", "pipe"],
  });
  children.add(child);
  prefixOutput(child, label);
  child.once("error", (error) => {
    process.stderr.write(`[${label}] ${error.message}\n`);
    void stopAll(1);
  });
  child.once("exit", (code, signal) => {
    children.delete(child);
    if (!stopping) {
      process.stderr.write(
        `[${label}] exited with ${signal ? `signal ${signal}` : `code ${code ?? "unknown"}`}\n`,
      );
      void stopAll(code && code > 0 ? code : 1);
    }
  });
  return child;
}

uuid: a9c09adc-d54e-4019-9b22-5bbe26f2afcd
parent: 0abdccc2-6bef-4fb8-851f-436cbbd8a95a

1	export function normalizeProjectPath(projectPath: string) {
2	  return projectPath.trim().replace(/[/\\]+$/, "") || "/";
3	}
4	
5	function pathSeparatorFor(projectPath: string) {
6	  return projectPath.includes("\\") && !projectPath.includes("/") ? "\\" : "/";
7	}
8	
9	export function isSameOrDescendantPath(pathValue: string, rootValue: string) {
10	  const path = normalizeProjectPath(pathValue);
11	  const root = normalizeProjectPath(rootValue);
12	  if (path === root) {
13	    return true;
14	  }
15	
16	  return path.startsWith(`${root}${pathSeparatorFor(root)}`);
17	}
18	
19	export function normalizeExplicitProjectPaths(explicitProjects: string[]) {
20	  return Array.from(new Set(
21	    explicitProjects
22	      .map((projectPath) => normalizeProjectPath(projectPath))
23	      .filter((projectPath) => projectPath.length > 0),
24	  ));
25	}
26	
27	export function resolveStaleProjectFallback(
28	  explicitProjects: string[],
29	  storedProjectPaths: Array<string | null | undefined>,
30	) {
31	  const normalizedExplicitProjects = normalizeExplicitProjectPaths(explicitProjects);
32	  if (normalizedExplicitProjects.length === 0) {
33	    return null;
34	  }
35	
36	  const directRunCounts = new Map(normalizedExplicitProjects.map((projectPath) => [projectPath, 0]));
37	  let hasUnmatchedStoredPath = false;
38	
39	  for (const storedProjectPath of storedProjectPaths) {
40	    const storedPath = storedProjectPath?.trim()
41	      ? normalizeProjectPath(storedProjectPath)
42	      : null;
43	    if (!storedPath) {
44	      continue;
45	    }
46	
47	    const matchingExplicitProject = normalizedExplicitProjects.find((projectPath) => (
48	      isSameOrDescendantPath(storedPath, projectPath)
49	    ));
50	
51	    if (matchingExplicitProject) {
52	      directRunCounts.set(matchingExplicitProject, (directRunCounts.get(matchingExplicitProject) ?? 0) + 1);
53	    } else {
54	      hasUnmatchedStoredPath = true;
55	    }
56	  }
57	
58	  if (!hasUnmatchedStoredPath) {
59	    return null;
60	  }
61	
62	  const emptyExplicitProjects = normalizedExplicitProjects.filter((projectPath) => (
63	    (directRunCounts.get(projectPath) ?? 0) === 0
64	  ));
65	
66	  return emptyExplicitProjects.length === 1 ? emptyExplicitProjects[0] : null;
67	}
68	
69	export function resolveStoredProjectRoot(
70	  storedProjectPath: string | null | undefined,
71	  explicitProjects: string[],
72	  options: { staleFallbackProject?: string | null } = {},
73	) {
74	  const normalizedExplicitProjects = normalizeExplicitProjectPaths(explicitProjects);
75	  const storedPath = storedProjectPath?.trim()
76	    ? normalizeProjectPath(storedProjectPath)
77	    : null;
78	
79	  if (!storedPath) {
80	    return null;
81	  }
82	
83	  const matchingExplicitProject = normalizedExplicitProjects.find((projectPath) => (
84	    isSameOrDescendantPath(storedPath, projectPath)
85	  ));
86	  if (matchingExplicitProject) {
87	    return matchingExplicitProject;
88	  }
89	
90	  if (normalizedExplicitProjects.length === 0) {
91	    return storedPath;
92	  }
93	
94	  if (normalizedExplicitProjects.length === 1) {
95	    return normalizedExplicitProjects[0];
96	  }
97	
98	  if (options.staleFallbackProject) {
99	    return normalizeProjectPath(options.staleFallbackProject);
100	  }
101	
102	  return null;
103	}

 route-aware, never enables credentialed wildcard requests, and never turns the
 password endpoint into an open cross-origin login API. Browser-to-browser login
 must use the approval/PKCE flow.
+
+## Filesystem roots
+
+`/api/fs`, `/api/fs/directories`, and `/api/fs/files` browse, create in, and read
+from an allowlist of directory trees. The default is the parent of the runner's
+working directory. `OMNIHARNESS_FS_ROOTS` replaces that default with a
+`path.delimiter`-separated list, which is how a project on a second drive becomes
+reachable:
+
+```
+OMNIHARNESS_FS_ROOTS=/Users/you/code:/Volumes/External/code
+```
+
+Every root is a full read surface for anyone holding a session — the project file
+walker descends into `.env` on purpose — so list the trees you keep code in
+rather than `/`. Paths outside every root are clamped back to the first readable
+root instead of being served. Directory creation resolves symlinks and rechecks
+containment against the canonical path, so a pre-existing link inside a root
+cannot redirect a `mkdir` outside it. A root that is not mounted stays in the
+allowlist and is reported to the folder picker as unavailable; it does not
+silently drop out and reroute the caller.

## Claude · 2026-08-27T09:46:39.192Z

Multi-root is in. The single hardcoded root is gone.

**What changed**

`src/server/fs/allowed-roots.ts` (new) holds the allowlist, read from `OMNIHARNESS_FS_ROOTS` on every call, `path.delimiter`-separated, defaulting to the old `cwd/..` so nothing changes for anyone who doesn't set it. It mirrors the `OMNIHARNESS_AGENT_SCRATCH_ROOTS` pattern already in `src/server/planning/artifacts.ts:11`.

`src/runtime/http/routes/filesystem.ts` now resolves against whichever root contains the path. Three behaviors worth naming:

- Out-of-root paths clamp to the first *readable* root, so an unmounted drive listed first doesn't brick the picker.
- `parent` stops at the containing root. With nested roots the outermost match wins, so browsing up from `/a/b` into an also-allowed `/a` isn't treated as an escape.
- `mkdir` still does the realpath recheck, now against the matched root — the existing symlink test passes unchanged.

The browse response gained `root` and `roots`. `FolderPickerDialog.tsx` renders a root switcher when there's more than one, since the dialog only had `..` and children — you could have navigated into Lexar and never gotten back out. Unmounted roots render disabled rather than throwing ENOENT at you. New keys `folder.picker.rootsLabel` and `folder.picker.rootUnavailable` are translated in all nine locales.

**Verified against your actual drive** by driving the real route with your `.env` loaded:

```
default root: /Users/masterman/NLP
roots: [{"path":"/Users/masterman/NLP","available":true},{"path":"/Volumes/Lexar/NLP","available":true}]
lexar status: 200 current: /Volumes/Lexar/NLP root: /Volumes/Lexar/NLP
lexar dirs: [aiminions-data, authorforge, baton, clonetools, ...]
/etc clamped to: /Users/masterman/NLP
```

I appended the var to your `.env` (gitignored):

```
OMNIHARNESS_FS_ROOTS=/Users/masterman/NLP:/Volumes/Lexar/NLP
```

**You need to restart the runner** — the process on port 3050 started before that line existed, and `--env-file-if-exists` only reads at boot. I left it running rather than killing it out from under you.

**Tests:** 7 new route tests in `tests/runtime/filesystem-allowed-roots.test.ts`, 2 new picker assertions, all passing along with the existing filesystem and i18n suites. Typecheck, eslint, and `pnpm build:interface:web` are clean.

Three failures in the wider `tests/runtime` + `tests/ui` run are pre-existing and unrelated to this work — `route-contract-fixture` and `next-standalone-route-parity` are out of date on five `/api/accounts/*` routes, and `terminal-fit` asserts on `Terminal.tsx` styling. I didn't touch any of those files.

One thing I did not do: the `PROJECTS` setting and `src/lib/project-paths.ts` are pure string matching with no filesystem check, so a run whose `projectPath` is on Lexar keeps resolving normally while the drive is unmounted rather than getting rebased onto another project. That seemed right, but it means an unmounted project will fail later at the agent rather than earlier in the UI.
